People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Since my daughter left Georgia for the military... that’s when I noticed this whole change in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I used to fool with my tomato plants, do a little sanding at my workbench, watch the Falcons… stay around after church and talk some. Lately I just kind of look at all that like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel pleasure when you do things, even if you've lost interest in them?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. Even if I try… the tomato plants, sanding a piece of wood, watching the Falcons… it just feels flat. Like it’s all somebody else’s stuff, not mine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve been slower, I guess. Folks at work keep asking if I’m sick. I’m walking slower, and even answering simple questions takes me a while. Feels like my body’s moving through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would make that happen, if you weren't going to wake up?\n\nPatient: I… I haven’t really gotten into how. That scared me enough I told my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant weight loss or gain since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been keeping track of the scale. But… most days I have to make myself eat a few bites. Food I usually like, at home or at the warehouse, it just sits there till I throw it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly about my daughter leaving. I keep turning it over in my head, thinking I must’ve failed her somehow. Like if I’d been a better father, maybe I would’ve done something different.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at work mostly. I’ll be looking at the schedule or a routing sheet and I just… can’t get my head to stay on it. Even small staffing decisions feel harder than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety. More the opposite, I guess. People at work keep asking if I’m sick because I’m moving so slow… taking too long to answer simple questions. Feels like my body’s in mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm besides not waking up?\n\nPatient: Not… not really more than that. It’s been those thoughts, like my family might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That’s what scared me enough to tell my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently bad?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty steady, I guess. This whole change in me’s been going on about two weeks… since my daughter left Georgia for the military.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Since my daughter left Georgia for the military... that’s when I noticed this whole change in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I used to fool with my tomato plants, do a little sanding at my workbench, watch the Falcons… stay around after church and talk some. Lately I just kind of look at all that like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel pleasure when you do things, even if you've lost interest in them?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. Even if I try… the tomato plants, sanding a piece of wood, watching the Falcons… it just feels flat. Like it’s all somebody else’s stuff, not mine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve been slower, I guess. Folks at work keep asking if I’m sick. I’m walking slower, and even answering simple questions takes me a while. Feels like my body’s moving through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would make that happen, if you weren't going to wake up?\n\nPatient: I… I haven’t really gotten into how. It’s more this thought that keeps coming up… that maybe my family’d be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That scared me enough I told my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant weight loss or gain since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been keeping track of the scale. But… most days I have to make myself eat a few bites. Food I usually like, at home or at the warehouse, it just sits there till I throw it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly about my daughter leaving. I keep turning it over in my head, thinking I must’ve failed her somehow. Like if I’d been a better father, maybe I would’ve done something different.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at work mostly. I’ll be looking at the schedule or a routing sheet and I just… can’t get my head to stay on it. Even small staffing decisions feel harder than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety. More the opposite, I guess. People at work keep asking if I’m sick because I’m moving so slow… taking too long to answer simple questions. Feels like my body’s in mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm besides not waking up?\n\nPatient: Not… not really more than that. It’s been those thoughts, like my family might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That’s what scared me enough to tell my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently bad?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty steady, I guess. Maybe little moments, but not really a good day. This whole change in me’s been going on about two weeks… since my daughter left Georgia for the military.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of actually taking action to end your life?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t made a plan or tried to do anything. It’s more just… I keep thinking maybe they’d be better off if I didn’t wake up. Like if I’d been a better father, maybe I would’ve done something different.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at work mostly. I’ll be looking at the schedule or a routing sheet and I just… can’t get my head to stay on it. Even small staffing decisions feel harder than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety. More the opposite, I guess. People at work keep asking if I’m sick because I’m moving so slow… taking too long to answer simple questions. Feels like my body’s in mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm besides not waking up?\n\nPatient: Not… not really more than that. It’s been those thoughts, like my family might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That’s what scared me enough to tell my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently bad?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty steady, I guess. Maybe little moments, but not really a good day. This whole change in me’s been going on about two weeks… since my daughter left Georgia for the military.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of actually taking action to end your life?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t made a plan or tried to do anything. It’s more just… I keep thinking maybe they’d be better off if I didn’t wake up. That scared me enough that I told my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I keep going over my daughter leaving and thinking I must’ve failed her somehow. Like… a better father would’ve done something different.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you are a burden to others besides thinking your family would be better off without you?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s that… I keep thinking I failed my daughter somehow. Like if I’d been a better father, maybe things would’ve been different. I don’t know. It’s hard not to see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty getting out of bed in the morning since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. Mornings aren’t great, but I can usually get myself up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Feels like my body’s moving through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would make that happen, if you weren't going to wake up?\n\nPatient: I… I haven’t really gotten into how. It’s more this thought that keeps coming up… that maybe my family’d be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That scared me enough I told my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant weight loss or gain since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been keeping track of the scale. But… most days I have to make myself eat a few bites. Food I usually like, at home or at the warehouse, it just sits there till I throw it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly about my daughter leaving. I keep turning it over in my head, thinking I must’ve failed her somehow. Like if I’d been a better father, maybe I would’ve done something different.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at work mostly. I’ll be looking at the schedule or a routing sheet and I just… can’t get my head to stay on it. Even small staffing decisions feel harder than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety. More the opposite, I guess. People at work keep asking if I’m sick because I’m moving so slow… taking too long to answer simple questions. Feels like my body’s in mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm besides not waking up?\n\nPatient: Not… not really more than that. It’s been those thoughts, like my family might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That’s what scared me enough to tell my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently bad?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty steady, I guess. Maybe little moments, but not really a good day. This whole change in me’s been going on about two weeks… since my daughter left Georgia for the military.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of actually taking action to end your life?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t made a plan or tried to do anything. It’s more just… I keep thinking maybe they’d be better off if I didn’t wake up. That scared me enough that I told my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I keep going over my daughter leaving and thinking I must’ve failed her somehow. Like… a better father would’ve done something different.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you are a burden to others besides thinking your family would be better off without you?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s that… I keep thinking I failed my daughter somehow. Like if I’d been a better father, maybe things would’ve been different. I don’t know. It’s hard not to see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty getting out of bed in the morning since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. Mornings aren’t great, but I can usually get myself up.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely unable to take care of your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there’ve been a couple days like that, I guess. Work’s been rough. I made some mistakes bad enough that my supervisor found me sitting in my truck before dawn and they got occupational health involved.\n\nAt home, I’ve let bills and things pile up. Stuff I’d normally just handle… I haven’t been.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about or come up with a specific way to end your life?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t really had a specific way in mind. It’s more that I keep thinking my family might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That scared me enough that I told my supervisor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where your depressive symptoms were noticeably worse?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… it’s kind of been up and down. Some days feel heavier than others, but I haven’t really been keeping track.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt okay, not depressed?\n\nPatient: Not really. Since my daughter left Georgia for the military, it’s been… about two weeks now, I guess, and I haven’t felt like myself much in that time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
